\frac{M\rhd M^{\prime}}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\rhd{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(M^{\prime}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A})}{\texttt{v-imp}}

\frac{M\rhd M^{\prime}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(A,M)}{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\rhd\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(M^{\prime}\,\mathbf{a})}{\texttt{v-all}}

\frac{M\rhd M^{\prime}}{\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,M\rhd\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,M^{\prime}}{\texttt{fresh1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{M\rhd M^{\prime}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(M)}{\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,M\rhd M^{\prime}}{\texttt{fresh2}}

Remark B.3.

1.

If
\lambda x.\,M\rhd N

then
N
is of the form

N=\lambda x.\,M^{\prime}
and
M\rhd M^{\prime}
.

2.

If
\Lambda{a}.\,M\rhd N

then
N
is of the form

N=\Lambda{a}.\,M^{\prime}
and
M\rhd M^{\prime}
.

3.

If
\star\rhd N
then
N=\star
.

4.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\rhd N
then
N={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
.

Lemma B.4 (Reflexivity).

M\rhd M

Proof.

Straightforward by induction on
M
.
∎

Lemma B.5 (Substitution).

If
M\rhd M^{\prime}
and
N\rhd N^{\prime}

then
M\{x:=N\}\rhd M^{\prime}\{x:=N^{\prime}\}
.

Proof.

Straightforward by induction on the derivation of
M\rhd M^{\prime}
,
resorting to reflexivity (
lemma

B.4
) in the
var
case.
∎

Lemma B.6 (Type substitution).

If
M\rhd M^{\prime}

then
M\{a:=A\}\rhd M^{\prime}\{a:=A\}
.

Proof.

Straightforward by induction on the derivation of
M\rhd M^{\prime}
.
Note that in the
v-eig
case
we have that
M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\rhd\star=M^{\prime}

where
\mathbf{a}
is a eigenvariable so
\mathbf{a}\neq a
.
∎

Lemma B.7 (Tait–Martin-Löf technique).

1.

{\to}\subseteq{\rhd}

2.

{\rhd}\subseteq{\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}}

3.

Diamond property:
\lhd\,\rhd\subseteq\rhd\,\lhd

Proof.

For the first item, suppose that
M\to N
.
It is straightforward to show that
M\rhd N

by induction on
M

and resorting to reflexivity (
lemma

B.4
).
For the second item, suppose that
M\rhd N
.
It is straightforward to show that
M\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}N

by induction on the derivation of
M\rhd N
.

For the third item, suppose that

M_{1}\rhd M_{2}
and
M_{1}\rhd M_{3}

and let us show that there exists a term
M_{4}

such that
M_{2}\rhd M_{4}
and
M_{3}\rhd M_{4}
.
We proceed by induction on
M_{4}
.

If
M_{1}
is a
variable
(
M_{1}=x
)
or a
success
(
M_{1}=\star
),
it is immediate because
M_{1}=M_{2}=M_{3}
and it suffices
to take
M_{4}:=M_{1}
.

If
M_{1}
is a
term-abstraction
(
M_{1}=\lambda x.\,N_{1}
)
or a
type-abstraction
(
M_{1}=\Lambda{a}.\,N_{1}
),
because the reduction can only be internal to
N_{1}
,
and we may conclude by resorting to the i.h..

If
M_{1}
is a
term-application
(
M_{1}=N_{1}\,P_{1}
)
there are four cases (two of them being symmetric),
depending on whether
both steps are derived using
app1
,
both steps are derived using
app2
,
or one is derived using
app1
and the other one using
app2
:

1.

app1
/
app1
:
Straightforward resorting to the i.h..

2.

app2
/
app2
:
Then
N_{1}=\lambda x.\,N^{\prime}_{1}

where
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{2}
and
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{3}

and
P_{1}\rhd P_{2}
and
P_{1}\rhd P_{3}
.
By i.h. there exists
N^{\prime}_{4}

such that
N^{\prime}_{2}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}
and
N^{\prime}_{3}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}

and there exists
P_{4}

such that
P_{2}\rhd P_{4}

and
P_{3}\rhd P_{4}
.
Taking
M_{4}:=N^{\prime}_{4}\{x:=P^{\prime}_{4}\}

and applying
lemma

B.5
, we have that:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\lambda x.\,N^{\prime}_{1})\,P_{1}&\rhd&N^{\prime}_{2}\{x:=P_{2}\}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
N^{\prime}_{3}\{x:=P_{3}\}&\rhd&N^{\prime}_{4}\{x:=P_{4}\}\end{array}

3.

app1
/
app2
:
Then
N_{1}=\lambda x.\,N^{\prime}_{1}

where
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{2}
and
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{3}

and
P_{1}\rhd P_{2}
and
P_{1}\rhd P_{3}
.
By i.h. there exists
N^{\prime}_{4}

such that
N^{\prime}_{2}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}
and
N^{\prime}_{3}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}

and there exists
P_{4}

such that
P_{2}\rhd P_{4}

and
P_{3}\rhd P_{4}
.
Taking
M_{4}:=N^{\prime}_{4}\{x:=P_{4}\}

and applying
lemma

B.5
, we have that:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\lambda x.\,N^{\prime}_{1})\,P_{1}&\rhd&(\lambda x.\,N^{\prime}_{2})\,P_{2}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
N^{\prime}_{3}\{x:=P_{3}\}&\rhd&N^{\prime}_{4}\{x:=P_{4}\}\end{array}

If
M_{1}
is a
type-application
(
M_{1}=N_{1}\,A
)
there are four cases (two of them being symmetric),
depending on whether
both steps are derived using
tapp1
,
both steps are derived using
tapp2
,
or one is derived using
tapp1
and the other one using
tapp2
:

1.

tapp1
/
tapp1
:
Straightforward resorting to the i.h..

2.

tapp2
/
tapp2
:
Then
N_{1}=\Lambda{a}.\,N^{\prime}_{1}

where
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{2}
and
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{3}

By i.h. there exists
N^{\prime}_{4}

such that
N^{\prime}_{2}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}

and
N^{\prime}_{3}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}
.
Taking
M_{4}:=N^{\prime}_{4}\{a:=A\}

and applying
lemma

B.6
we have that:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\Lambda{a}.\,N^{\prime}_{1})\,A&\rhd&N^{\prime}_{2}\{a:=A\}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
N^{\prime}_{3}\{a:=A\}&\rhd&N^{\prime}_{4}\{a:=A\}\end{array}

3.

tapp1
/
tapp2
:
Then
N_{1}=\Lambda{a}.\,N^{\prime}_{1}

where
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{2}
and
N^{\prime}_{1}\rhd N^{\prime}_{3}
.
By i.h. there exists
N^{\prime}_{4}

such that
N^{\prime}_{2}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}

and
N^{\prime}_{3}\rhd N^{\prime}_{4}
.
Taking
M_{4}:=N^{\prime}_{4}\{a:=A\}

and applying
lemma

B.6
, we have that:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\Lambda{a}.\,N^{\prime}_{1})\,A&\rhd&(\Lambda{a}.\,N^{\prime}_{2})\,A\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
N^{\prime}_{3}\{a:=A\}&\rhd&N^{\prime}_{4}\{a:=A\}\end{array}

If
M_{1}
is a
guard
(
M_{1}=N_{1}{?}P_{1}
)
there are four cases (two of them being symmetric),
depending on whether
both steps are derived using
guard1
,
both steps are derived using
guard2
,
or one is derived using
guard1
and the other one using
guard2
:

1.

guard1
/
guard1
:
Straightforward resorting to the i.h..

2.

guard2
/
guard2
:
Then
N_{1}=\star

where
N_{2}=N_{3}=\star

and
P_{1}\rhd P_{2}
and
P_{1}\rhd P_{3}
.
By i.h. there exists
P_{4}

such that
P_{2}\rhd P_{4}

and
P_{3}\rhd P_{4}
.
Taking
M_{4}:=P_{4}
we have that:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\star{?}P_{1}&\rhd&P_{2}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
P_{3}&\rhd&P_{4}\end{array}

3.

guard1
/
guard2
:
Then
N_{1}=\star

where
N_{2}=N_{3}=\star

and
P_{1}\rhd P_{2}
and
P_{1}\rhd P_{3}
.
By i.h. there exists
P_{4}

such that
P_{2}\rhd P_{4}

and
P_{3}\rhd P_{4}
.
Taking
M_{4}:=P_{4}
, we have that:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\star{?}P_{1}&\rhd&\star{?}P_{2}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
P_{3}&\rhd&P_{4}\end{array}

If
M_{1}
is a
generator
(
M_{1}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}
),
note first that if both steps are derived using the same rule,
it is immediate to conclude
because
M_{2}=M_{3}
and it suffices to take
M_{4}:=M_{2}

and resort to reflexivity (
lemma

B.4
).
Moreover,
note that it is not possible to derive one step using the
g-imp
rule
and the other one using the
g-all
rule,
because we would simultaneously have
A=(B\Rightarrow C)

and
A=(\forall a.\,D)
, which is impossible.
The remaining cases are when
one step is derived using the
g-refl
rule
and the other one is derived using either the
g-imp

or the
g-all
rule. These cases are immediate
because if
M_{1}\rhd M_{2}
is derived using the
g-refl

rule then
M_{2}=M_{1}
, and it suffices to take
M_{4}:=M_{3}
.

If
M_{1}
is a
verifier
(
M_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N_{1}
),
note first that if both steps are derived using the same rule
it is immediate to conclude by resorting to the i.h..
Moreover, note that the rules
v-eig
,
v-imp
,
and
v-all
are mutually exclusive,
because
A
cannot be simultaneously of two shapes
(
e.g.
an eigenvariable and an implication).
The remaining cases are when
one step is derived using the
v-cong
rule
and the other one is derived using either the
v-eig
,
the
v-imp
, or the
v-all
rule:

1.

v-cong
/
v-eig
:
Then
M_{1}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
.
Suppose that the step
M_{1}\rhd M_{2}

is derived from the
v-cong
rule.
Then necessarily
M_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
,
and the situation is:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}&\rhd&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
\star&\rhd&\star\end{array}

2.

v-cong
/
v-imp
:
Then
A=(B\Rightarrow C)
.
Suppose that the step
M_{1}\rhd M_{2}

is derived from the
v-cong
rule
and
M_{1}\rhd M_{2}

is derived from the
v-imp
rule.
Then
M_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\Rightarrow C{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N_{2}

where
N_{1}\rhd N_{2}
,
and
M_{3}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N_{3}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B})

where
N_{1}\rhd N_{3}
.
By i.h. there exists
N_{4}

such that
N_{2}\rhd N_{4}

and
N_{3}\rhd N_{4}
,
and the situation is:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\Rightarrow C{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N_{1}&\rhd&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\Rightarrow C{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N_{2}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N_{3}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B})&\rhd&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N_{4}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B})\end{array}

3.

v-cong
/
v-all
:
Then
A=(\forall a.\,B)
.
Suppose that the step
M_{1}\rhd M_{2}

is derived from the
v-cong
rule
and
M_{1}\rhd M_{2}

is derived from the
v-all
rule.
Then
M_{2}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N_{2}

where
N_{1}\rhd N_{2}
,
and
M_{3}=\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N_{3}\,\mathbf{a})

where
N_{1}\rhd N_{3}
.
By i.h. there exists
N_{4}

such that
N_{2}\rhd N_{4}

and
N_{3}\rhd N_{4}
,
and the situation is:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N_{1}&\rhd&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N_{2}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N_{3}\,\mathbf{a})&\rhd&\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N_{4}\,\mathbf{a})\end{array}

If
M_{1}
is a
fresh eigenvariable introduction
(
M_{1}=\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,N_{1}
)
there are four cases (two of them being symmetric),
depending on whether
both steps are derived using
fresh1
,
both steps are derived using
fresh2
,
or one is derived using
fresh1
and the other one using
fresh2
:

1.

fresh1
/
fresh1
:
Straightforward resorting to the i.h..

2.

fresh2
/
fresh2
:
Straightforward resorting to the i.h..

3.

fresh1
/
fresh2
:
Then
N_{1}\rhd N_{2}

and
N_{1}\rhd N_{3}

and
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(N_{1})
.
By i.h. there exists
N_{4}

such that
N_{2}\rhd N_{4}

and
N_{3}\rhd N_{4}
,
and the situation is:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,N_{1}&\rhd&\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,N_{2}\\
\triangledown&&\triangledown\\
N_{3}&\rhd&N_{4}\end{array}

To be able to perform the reduction on the right,
observe that
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(N_{2})

because
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(N_{1})
and
N_{1}\rhd N_{2}
,
and reduction does not create free eigenvariables.

∎

Proposition B.8.

Reduction in
\lambda^{\mathsf{F}}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is confluent.

Proof.

An immediate consequence of
lemma

B.7

by abstract results
(
50
, Prop. 1.1.11)
.
∎

B.3.
Second-Order: Standardization (
proposition

4.4
)

We use a non-idempotent intersection type system
\mathcal{T}
to prove
standardization (
proposition

4.4
).

The sets of
linear-types
and
linear-multitypes
of
\mathcal{T}

are given mutually inductively by the grammar:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\textsc{Linear-types }&\mathbb{T},\mathbb{S},\ldots&::=&\star\\
&&\mid&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{a}\\
&&\mid&\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}\\
&&\mid&A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}\\
\textsc{Linear-multitypes }&\mathbb{M},\mathbb{N},\ldots&::=&[\mathbb{T}_{i}]_{i\in I}\end{array}

Linear-multitypes are finite multisets of linear-types.
The notation
[\mathbb{T}_{i}]_{i\in I}
stands for a multiset of elements
indexed by a finite set
I
, that sometimes we may also write
more explicitly as
[\mathbb{T}_{1},\ldots,\mathbb{T}_{n}]
.
The
size
of a linear-multitype
\mathbb{M}
is written
\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert

and defined as its cardinality as a multiset (
i.e.
number of elements,
counting their multiplicities).
We write
\mathbb{M}+\mathbb{N}
for the union of linear-multitypes.
A
linear-environment
is a function
\mathbf{E}
mapping each
variable to a linear-multitype. We assume that linear-environments have
finite support
,
i.e.
that
\mathbf{E}(x)\neq[\,]

for only finitely many variables.
If
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{F}
are linear-environments, we write
\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}

for the linear-environment such that

(\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F})(x)=\mathbf{E}(x)+\mathbf{F}(x)

for each variable
x
.

We define two forms of typing judgments,
\mathbf{E}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}

and
\mathbf{E}\Vdash M:\mathbb{M}
.
Valid judgments in system
\mathcal{T}
are defined inductively by
the following rules:

\frac{}{x:[\mathbb{T}]\vdash x:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lvar}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\lambda x.\,M:\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Llam}}}

\frac{\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash M:\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{E}_{2}\Vdash N:\mathbb{M}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash M\,N:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lapp}}}

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash M\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,M:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Llamt}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash M:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash M\,A:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lappt}}}

\frac{}{\vdash\star:\star}{\texttt{{L$\star$}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash M:\star\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash M{?}N:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L${?}$}}}

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(\mathbf{E},\mathbb{T})}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,M:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\nu$}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash M:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}$}}}

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\star}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}$}}}

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(M\,\mathbf{a}):\star\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(A,M)}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\forall}$}}}

\frac{}{\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese\mathbf{a}$}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\Rightarrow}$}}}

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\forall}$}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{(\mathbf{E}_{i}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}_{i})_{i\in I}}{+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i}\Vdash M:[\mathbb{T}_{i}]_{i\in I}}{\texttt{{Lmulti}}}

Derivation trees (or just
derivations
)
in this system are ranged over by
\Phi,\Psi,\ldots
.
We write
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}

to mean that
\Phi
is a derivation tree with conclusion
\mathbf{E}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}
.
The
size
of a derivation
\Phi
is written
\lvert\Phi\rvert

and defined as the number of inference rules in
\Phi
,
without counting instances of the
Lmulti
rule.

Lemma B.9 (Weighted substitution lemma).

If
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}

and
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\Vdash N:\mathbb{M}

where
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(N)
,
then there exists a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}

such that
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash M\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}

and
\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Phi
:

Case
Lvar
:

Let
\Phi\rhd y:[\mathbb{T}]\vdash y:\mathbb{T}
.
We consider two subcases, depending on whether
y=x
or not:

1.

If
M=y=x
,
the derivation
\Phi

is of the form
\Phi\rhd x:[\mathbb{T}]\vdash x:\mathbb{T}
,
so
\mathbf{E}=\varnothing

and
\mathbb{M}=[\mathbb{T}]
.
Moreover, the derivation
\Psi
is of the form

\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\Vdash N:[\mathbb{T}]

and it must be derived by
Lmulti
from a single premise

\Psi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{F}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}
.
Our goal is to construct a derivation with conclusion

\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash M\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
,
that is to say,
\mathbf{F}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}
.
It suffices to take
\Phi^{\prime}:=\Psi^{\prime}
.
Note that
\lvert\Phi\rvert=1
and
\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert=1
, so

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=\lvert\Psi^{\prime}\rvert=\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

2.

If
M=y\neq x
,
the derivation
\Phi

is of the form
\Phi\rhd y:[\mathbb{T}]\vdash y:\mathbb{T}
,
so
\mathbf{E}=(y:[\mathbb{T}])

and
\mathbb{M}=[\,]
.
Moreover, the derivation
\Psi

is of the form
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\Vdash N:[\,]

and it must be derived by
Lmulti
with the empty set of premises,
so in particular
\mathbf{F}=\varnothing

and
\lvert\Psi\rvert=0
.
Our goal is to construct a derivation with conclusion

\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash M\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
,
that is to say,
y:[\mathbb{T}]\vdash y:\mathbb{T}
.
It suffices to take
\Phi^{\prime}:=\Phi
.
Note that
\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert=0
, so

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
Llam
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M},y:\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\vdash P:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash\lambda y.\,P:\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\multimap\mathbb{T}^{\prime}}{\texttt{{Llam}}}

Assume by
\alpha
-conversion that
y\notin\mathsf{fv}(x,N)
.
By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd(\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M})+\mathbf{F}\vdash P\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}

such that
\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Note that
((\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M})+\mathbf{F})=((\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}),x:\mathbb{M})
.
Applying
Llam

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash\lambda y.\,P\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{N}^{\prime}\multimap\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
.
Moreover

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
Lapp
:

Let
\Phi
be derived using the
Lapp
rule.
Then it is necessarily of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{0}\\
\mathbf{E}_{0},x:\mathbb{M}_{0}\vdash P:[\mathbb{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}\multimap\mathbb{T}\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\Pi}{\mathbf{E}_{0}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash P\,Q:\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\multimap\mathbb{T}^{\prime}}{\texttt{{Lapp}}}

with

\Pi=\frac{\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{i}\\
\mathbf{E}_{i},x:\mathbb{M}_{i}\vdash Q:\mathbb{S}_{i}\end{array}\right)_{i\in I}}{+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i},x:+_{i\in I}\mathbb{M}_{i}\vdash Q:[\mathbb{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}}{\texttt{{Lmulti}}}

where
I
is a finite set,
and we assume that there is an index
0\notin I
,
and moreover
M=P\,Q

and
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{0}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i}

and
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}_{0}+_{i\in I}\mathbb{M}_{i}
.

The derivation
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\vdash N:\mathbb{M}

is derived from the
Lmulti
rule,
and thus it can be split in
|I|+1
derivations

\Psi_{i}\rhd\mathbf{F}_{i}\vdash N:\mathbb{M}_{i}
,
one for each index
i\in\{0\}\cup I
,
in such a way that

\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{F}_{0}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{F}_{i}

and
\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Psi_{0}\rvert+_{i\in I}\lvert\Psi_{i}\rvert
.

Applying the i.h. on
\Phi_{0}
and
\Psi_{0}

we have that there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{0}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{0}+\mathbf{F}_{0}\vdash P\{x:=N\}:[\mathbb{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}\multimap\mathbb{T}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{0}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{0}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{0}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{0}\rvert
.

Applying the i.h. for each
i\in I
on
\Phi_{i}
and
\Psi_{i}

we have that there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{i}+\mathbf{F}_{i}\vdash Q\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{S}_{i}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{i}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{i}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{i}\rvert
.

Hence we may construct
\Phi^{\prime}
as follows:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}_{0}\\
\mathbf{E}_{0}+\mathbf{F}_{0}\vdash P\{x:=N\}:[\mathbb{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}\multimap\mathbb{T}\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\Pi^{\prime}}{\mathbf{E}_{0}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i}+\mathbf{F}_{0}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{F}_{i}\vdash P\{x:=N\}\,Q\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\multimap\mathbb{T}^{\prime}}{\texttt{{Lapp}}}

with

\Pi^{\prime}:=\frac{\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\\
\mathbf{E}_{i}+\mathbf{F}_{i}\vdash Q\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{S}_{i}\end{array}\right)_{i\in I}}{+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{F}_{i}\vdash Q\{x:=N\}:[\mathbb{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}}{\texttt{{Lmulti}}}

To conclude, note that
\lvert\Phi\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{0}\rvert+_{i\in I}\lvert\Phi_{i}\rvert
,
so

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{0}\rvert+_{i\in I}\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\rvert=1+(\lvert\Phi_{0}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{0}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{0}\rvert)+_{i\in I}(\lvert\Phi_{i}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{i}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{i}\rvert)=1+\lvert\Phi_{0}\rvert+_{i\in I}\lvert\Phi_{i}\rvert-(\lvert\mathbb{M}_{0}\rvert+_{i\in I}\lvert\mathbb{M}_{i}\rvert)+(\lvert\Psi_{0}\rvert+_{i\in I}\lvert\Psi_{i}\rvert)=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
Llamt
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash P\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,P:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}}{\texttt{{Llamt}}}

where by
\alpha
-conversion we may assume that
a\notin\mathsf{fv}(N)
.
By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash P\{a:=A\}\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Note that

P\{a:=A\}\{x:=N\}=P\{x:=N\}\{a:=A\}

because
a\notin\mathsf{fv}(N)
.
Applying
Llamt

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash\forall a.\,P\{x:=N\}:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
.
Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
Lappt
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash P:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash P\,A:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lappt}}}

By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash P\{x:=N\}:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Applying
Lappt

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash P\{x:=N\}\,A:\mathbb{T}
.
Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
\star
:

Let
\Phi
be derived from
L
\star
.
Then
\Phi\rhd\vdash\star:\star

where
\mathbf{E}=\varnothing

and
\mathbb{M}=[\,]

and
\mathbb{T}=\star
.
Note that the derivation
\Psi

is of the form
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\Vdash N:[\,]

and it must be derived by
Lmulti
with the empty set of premises,
so
\mathbf{F}=\varnothing
and
\lvert\Psi\rvert=0
.
Our goal is to construct a derivation with conclusion

\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash M\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
,
that is to say,
\vdash\star:\star
.
It suffices to take
\Phi^{\prime}:=\Phi
,
noting that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
{?}
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E}_{1},x:\mathbb{M}_{1}\vdash P:\star\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{2}\\
\mathbf{E}_{2},x:\mathbb{M}_{2}\vdash Q:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2},x:\mathbb{M}_{1}+\mathbb{M}_{2}\vdash P{?}Q:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L${?}$}}}

where
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}

and
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}_{1}+\mathbb{M}_{2}

and
M=P{?}Q
.
Note that the derivation
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\vdash N:\mathbb{M}

is derived from the
Lmulti
rule,
so it can be split into

\Psi_{1}\rhd\mathbf{F}_{1}\vdash N:\mathbb{M}_{1}

and

\Psi_{2}\rhd\mathbf{F}_{2}\vdash N:\mathbb{M}_{2}
,
where

\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{F}_{1}+\mathbf{F}_{2}

and
\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Psi_{1}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{2}\rvert
.
By i.h. on
\Phi_{1}
,
there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{F}_{1}\vdash P\{x:=N\}:\star

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{1}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{1}\rvert
.
By i.h. on
\Phi_{2}
,
there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{2}+\mathbf{F}_{2}\vdash Q\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{2}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{2}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{2}\rvert
.
Applying
L
{?}
on
\Phi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\Phi^{\prime}_{2}

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}+\mathbf{F}_{1}+\mathbf{F}_{2}\vdash P\{x:=N\}{?}Q\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
.
To conclude, note that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{2}\rvert=1+(\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{1}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{1}\rvert)+(\lvert\Phi_{2}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}_{2}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{2}\rvert)=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert+\lvert\Phi_{2}\rvert-(\lvert\mathbb{M}_{1}\rvert+\lvert\mathbb{M}_{2}\rvert)+(\lvert\Psi_{1}\rvert+\lvert\Psi_{2}\rvert)=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
\nu
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash P:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,P:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\nu$}}}

where
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(\mathbf{E},\mathbb{M},\mathbb{T})
.
By
\alpha
-conversion, we may assume also that
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(\mathbf{F},N)
.
By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash P\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Applying
L
\nu

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,P\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
.
Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash P:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}P:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}$}}}

By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash P\{x:=N\}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Applying
L
\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}P\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
.
Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(P\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\star\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}P:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}$}}}

By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(P\{x:=N\}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\star

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Applying
L
\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}P\{x:=N\}:\star

Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
\langle\rangle{\forall}
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(P\,\mathbf{a}):\star}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}P:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\forall}$}}}

where
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(A,P)
.
By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(P\{x:=N\}\,\mathbf{a}):\star

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Applying
L
\langle\rangle{\forall}

we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}P\{x:=N\}:\star
.
Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
\maltese\mathbf{a}
:

Let
\Phi
be derived from
L
\maltese\mathbf{a}
.
Then
\Phi\rhd\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
,
where
\mathbf{E}=\varnothing

and
\mathbb{M}=[\,]

and
\mathbb{T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
.
Note that the derivation
\Psi

is of the form
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\Vdash N:[\,]

and it must be derived by
Lmulti
with the empty set of premises,
so
\mathbf{F}=\varnothing
and
\lvert\Psi\rvert=0
.
Our goal is to construct a derivation with conclusion

\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash M\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
,
that is to say,
\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
.
It suffices to take
\Phi^{\prime}:=\Phi
,
noting that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Case
L
\maltese{\Rightarrow}
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash\lambda y.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}y{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\Rightarrow}$}}}

By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash\lambda y.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}y{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}:\mathbb{T}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Applying
L
\maltese{\Rightarrow}
we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}:\mathbb{T}
.
Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.

Note:
in this case, one can argue that necessarily

\mathbf{E}=\varnothing
and
\mathbb{M}=[\,]
,
and consequently
\mathbf{F}=\varnothing
,
but we do not need to make this remark for our proof to go through.

Case
L
\maltese{\forall}
:

Let
\Phi
be of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\forall}$}}}

By i.h. there exists a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}:\mathbb{T}

such that

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
Applying
L
\maltese{\Rightarrow}
we obtain a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}+\mathbf{F}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}:\mathbb{T}
.
Moreover,

\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert=\lvert\Phi\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Psi\rvert
.
∎

Lemma B.10 (Weak head subject reduction).

Let
M\to_{w}M^{\prime}
be a weak head reduction step.
If
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}
,
then there exists a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}

such that
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}

and
\lvert\Phi\rvert>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
\Phi
.

Case
Lvar
:

Then
M=x
which is
\to_{w}
-irreducible.

Case
Llam
:

Then
M=\lambda x.\,N
, which is
\to_{w}
-irreducible.

Case
Lapp
:

Then
M=N\,P
.
If the reduction is internal to
N
, it is immediate to conclude by i.h..
So we may assume that the reduction is at the root.
Then
N=\lambda x.\,Q

and the step is of the form

M=(\lambda x.\,Q)\,P\to_{w}Q\{x:=P\}=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E}_{1},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash Q:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash\lambda x.\,Q:\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Llam}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{2}\\
\mathbf{E}_{2}\Vdash P:\mathbb{M}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash(\lambda x.\,Q)\,P:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lapp}}}

where
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}

and where we may assume by
\alpha
-conversion that
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(P)
.
By the weighted substitution lemma (
lemma

B.9
)
there exists a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}

such that
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash Q\{x:=P\}:\mathbb{T}

where

\lvert\Phi\rvert=\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert+\lvert\Phi_{2}\rvert+2>\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert-\lvert\mathbb{M}\rvert+\lvert\Phi_{2}\rvert=\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
Llamt
:

Then
M=\Lambda{a}.\,N
, which is
\to_{w}
-irreducible.

Case
Lappt
:

Then
M=N\,A
.
If the reduction is internal to
N
, it is immediate to conclude by i.h..
So we may assume that the reduction is at the root.
Then
N=\Lambda{a}.\,P

and the step is of the form

M=(\Lambda{a}.\,P)\,A\to_{w}P\{a:=A\}=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{E}\vdash P\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,P:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Llamt}}}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash(\Lambda{a}.\,P)\,A:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lappt}}}

To conclude, note that

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash P\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}

and

\lvert\Phi\rvert=\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert+2>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
L
\star
:

Then
M=\star
, which is
\to_{w}
-irreducible.

Case
L
{?}
:

Then
M=N{?}P
.
If the reduction is internal to
N
, it is immediate to conclude by i.h..
So we may assume that the reduction is at the root.
Then
N=\star

and the step is of the form

M=\star{?}P\to_{w}P=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\frac{}{\vdash\star:\star}{\texttt{{L$\star$}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash P:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash\star{?}P:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L${?}$}}}

where
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}
.
To conclude, note that

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash P:\mathbb{T}

and

\lvert\Phi\rvert=\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert+2>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
L
\nu
:

Then
M=\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,N
.
If the reduction is internal to
N
, it is immediate to conclude by i.h..
So we may assume that the reduction is at the root.
Then
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(N)

and the step is of the form

M=\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,N\to_{w}N=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{E}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(\mathbf{E},\mathbb{T})}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,N:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\nu$}}}

To conclude, note that

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}

and

\lvert\Phi\rvert=\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert+1>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
L
\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}
:

Then
M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N
.
If the reduction is internal to
N
,
it is immediate to conclude by i.h..
So we may assume that the reduction is at the root.
Then
N={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}

and the step is of the form

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N\to_{w}\star=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\frac{}{\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese\mathbf{a}$}}}}{\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}$}}}

where in particular the context
\mathbf{E}
must necessarily be empty.
Define
\Phi^{\prime}
as the derivation constructed using the
rule
L
\star
,
so that
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\vdash\star:\star
.
To conclude, note that

\lvert\Phi\rvert=2>1=\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
L
\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}
:

Then
M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N
.
If the reduction is internal to
N
,
it is immediate to conclude by i.h..
So we may assume that the reduction is at the root.
Then the step is of the form

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N\to_{w}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A})=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\star\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}$}}}

To conclude, note that

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\star

and

\lvert\Phi\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
L
\langle\rangle{\forall}
:

Then
M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N
.
If the reduction is internal to
N
,
it is immediate to conclude by i.h..
So we may assume that the reduction is at the root.
Then the step is of the form

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N\to_{w}\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a})=M^{\prime}
,
where
\mathbf{a}
is assumed to be fresh,
i.e.

\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(A,N)
,
and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a}):\star\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(A,N)}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\forall}$}}}

To conclude, note that

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a}):\star

and

\lvert\Phi\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
L
\maltese\mathbf{a}
:

Then
M={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
, which is
\to_{w}
-irreducible.

Case
L
\maltese{\Rightarrow}
:

Then
M={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}
,
the step is of the form

M={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}\to_{w}\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{E}\vdash\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\Rightarrow}$}}}

To conclude, note that

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}:\mathbb{T}

and

\lvert\Phi\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.

Case
L
\maltese{\forall}
:

Then
M={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}
,
the step is of the form

M={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}\to_{w}\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}=M^{\prime}

and the derivation is of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\forall}$}}}

To conclude, note that

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}:\mathbb{T}

and

\lvert\Phi\rvert=1+\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert>\lvert\Phi^{\prime}\rvert
.
∎

Lemma B.11 (Anti-substitution lemma).

If
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}
,
then there exist linear-environments
\mathbf{E}_{0},\mathbf{F}

and a linear-multitype
\mathbb{M}

such that
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{0}+\mathbf{F}

and
\Phi_{0}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{0},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}

and
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\Vdash N:\mathbb{M}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\Phi
.
We start by considering the case in which
M=x
.
In such case, we have by hypothesis
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}
.
So it suffices to take

\mathbf{E}_{0}:=\varnothing

and
\mathbf{F}:=\mathbf{E}

and
\mathbb{M}:=[\mathbb{T}]
,
with the derivation

\Phi_{0}\rhd x:[\mathbb{T}]\vdash x:\mathbb{T}

constructed applying
Lvar
,
and

\Psi\rhd\mathbf{E}\Vdash N:[\mathbb{T}]

constructed applying
Lmulti
, with
\Phi
as the single premise.

Now, in the remainder of the proof, we may assume that
M\neq x
.
We proceed by case analysis on the last rule applied to construct
\Phi
.
The proof in each case is similar to the weighted substitution lemma (
lemma

B.9
),
but reasoning backwards.
For example, in the case of rule
L
{?}
,
we have that
M\{x:=N\}
is of the form
P{?}Q
,
and since
M\neq x

we have that
M=P_{0}{?}Q_{0}

with
P_{0}\{x:=N\}=P

and
Q_{0}\{x:=N\}=Q
.
The derivation
\Phi
is of the form:

\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{1}\\
\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash P_{0}\{x:=N\}:\star\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi_{2}\\
\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash Q_{0}\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}\end{array}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash P_{0}\{x:=N\}{?}Q_{0}\{x:=N\}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L${?}$}}}

where
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}
.
By i.h. on
\Phi_{1}
we have that there exist
\mathbf{E}_{10},\mathbf{F}_{1},\mathbb{M}_{1}

such that
\mathbf{E}_{1}=\mathbf{E}_{10}+\mathbf{F}_{1}

and there exist derivations

\Phi_{10}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{10},x:\mathbb{M}_{1}\vdash P_{0}:\star

and
\Psi_{1}\rhd\mathbf{F}_{1}\Vdash N:\mathbb{M}_{1}
.
Similarly, by i.h. on
\Phi_{2}
we have that there exist
\mathbf{E}_{20},\mathbf{F}_{2},\mathbb{M}_{2}

such that
\mathbf{E}_{2}=\mathbf{E}_{20}+\mathbf{F}_{2}

and there exist derivations

\Phi_{20}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{20},x:\mathbb{M}_{2}\vdash Q_{0}:\mathbb{T}

and
\Psi_{2}\rhd\mathbf{F}_{2}\Vdash N:\mathbb{M}_{2}
.

To conclude, take

\mathbf{E}_{0}:=\mathbf{E}_{10}+\mathbf{E}_{20}

and
\mathbf{F}:=\mathbf{F}_{1}+\mathbf{F}_{2}

and
\mathbb{M}:=\mathbb{M}_{1}+\mathbb{M}_{2}
.
Note that

\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}=\mathbf{E}_{10}+\mathbf{F}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{20}+\mathbf{F}_{2}=\mathbf{E}_{0}+\mathbf{F}
.
Applying
L
{?}
on the derivations
\Phi_{10}
and
\Phi_{20}

we obtain a derivation

\Phi_{0}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{10}+\mathbf{E}_{20},x:\mathbb{M}_{1}+\mathbb{M}_{2}\vdash P_{0}{?}Q_{0}:\mathbb{T}
,
that is,

\Phi_{0}\rhd\mathbf{E}_{0},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}
.
Moreover, the derivations
\Psi_{1}
and
\Psi_{2}
must be derived using

Lmulti
, so they can be joined to obtain a derivation

\Psi_{1}+\Psi_{2}\rhd\mathbf{F}_{1}+\mathbf{F}_{2}\Vdash N:\mathbb{M}_{1}+\mathbb{M}_{2}
,
that is,
\Psi\rhd\mathbf{F}\Vdash N:\mathbb{M}
.
∎

Lemma B.12 (Subject expansion).

Let
M\to M^{\prime}
.
If
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
,
then
\mathbf{E}\vdash M:\mathbb{T}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the size of
\Phi
.
We consider cases depending on the way in which the step
M\to M^{\prime}

is derived,
including cases for root reduction
(given by an application of a rewriting rule at the root of
M
)
as well as congruence closure
(given by reducing internally to a subterm).

Root reduction cases.

1.

Suppose that

M=(\lambda x.\,N)\,P\to N\{x:=P\}=M^{\prime}
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash N\{x:=P\}:\mathbb{T}
.
By the anti-substitution lemma (
lemma

B.11
)
there exist
\mathbf{E}_{0},\mathbf{F},\mathbb{M}
such that

\mathbf{E}_{0},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}

and
\mathbf{F}\Vdash P:\mathbb{M}
.
Hence:

\frac{\frac{\mathbf{E}_{0},x:\mathbb{M}\vdash N:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}_{0}\vdash\lambda x.\,N:\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Llam}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{F}\Vdash P:\mathbb{M}}{\mathbf{E}_{0}+\mathbf{F}\vdash(\lambda x.\,N)\,P:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lapp}}}

2.

Suppose that

M=(\Lambda{a}.\,N)\,A\to N\{a:=A\}=M^{\prime}
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash N\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}
.
Hence:

\frac{\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash N\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,N:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Llamt}}}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash(\Lambda{a}.\,N)\,A:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lappt}}}

3.

Suppose that

M=\star{?}M^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash\star{?}M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
.
Hence:

\frac{\frac{}{\vdash\star:\star}{\texttt{{L$\star$}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\star{?}M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L${?}$}}}

4.

Suppose that

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\to\star=M^{\prime}
:
Note that
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}

has the judgment
\mathbf{E}\vdash\star:\mathbb{T}
as its conclusion,
which only be derived using
L
\star

with
\mathbf{E}=\varnothing
and
\mathbb{T}=\star
.
To conclude, note that:

\frac{\frac{}{\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese\mathbf{a}$}}}}{\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle\mathbf{a}$}}}

5.

Suppose that

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N\to{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A})=M^{\prime}
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\mathbb{T}
.
Hence:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}$}}}

6.

Suppose that

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N\to\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a})=M^{\prime}
,
where
\mathbf{a}
is fresh,
i.e.

\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(A,N)
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a}):\mathbb{T}
.
Hence:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a}):\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\forall}$}}}

7.

Suppose that

M={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}\to\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}=M^{\prime}
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}:\mathbb{T}
.
Hence:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\Rightarrow}$}}}

8.

Suppose that

M={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}\to\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}=M^{\prime}
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}:\mathbb{T}
.
Hence:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,A}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\maltese{\forall}$}}}

9.

Suppose that

M=\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,M^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
,
where
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(M^{\prime})
:
Then by hypothesis

\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
.
By
\alpha
-conversion, we may suppose that

\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(\mathbf{E},\mathbb{T})
.
Hence:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{L$\nu$}}}

Congruence closure cases.

Most congruence closure cases are straightforward by resorting to the i.h.;
we only include the proofs of the most illustrative or interesting cases.

10.

Left of a term-application,

M=N\,P\to N^{\prime}\,P=M^{\prime}

with
N\to N^{\prime}
.
The derivation
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
is of the form:

\frac{\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash N^{\prime}:\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{E}_{2}\Vdash P:\mathbb{M}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash N^{\prime}\,P:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lapp}}}

where
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}
.
Since
N\to N^{\prime}
, by i.h. we have that

\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash N:\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}
,
hence applying the rule
Lapp

we obtain that
\mathbf{E}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{2}\vdash N\,P:\mathbb{T}
.

11.

Right of a term-application,

M=N\,P\to N\,P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}

with
P\to P^{\prime}
.
The derivation
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
is of the form:

\frac{\mathbf{E}_{1}\vdash N:\mathbb{M}\multimap\mathbb{T}\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{(\mathbf{E}_{2,i}\Vdash P^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}_{i})_{i\in I}}{+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{2,i}\Vdash P^{\prime}:[\mathbb{T}_{i}]_{i\in I}}{\texttt{{Lmulti}}}}{\mathbf{E}_{1}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{2,i}\vdash N\,P^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}}{\texttt{{Lapp}}}

where
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{1}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{2,i}
.
Since
P\to P^{\prime}
, applying the i.h. once per each
{i\in I}

we have that
\mathbf{E}_{2,i}\vdash P:\mathbb{T}_{i}
holds for each
{i\in I}
.
Hence applying the rules
Lmulti
and
Lapp

we obtain that
\mathbf{E}_{1}+_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{2,i}\vdash N\,P:\mathbb{T}
,
as required.

12.

Inside a type-abstraction,

M=\Lambda{a}.\,N\to\Lambda{a}.\,N^{\prime}=M^{\prime}

with
N\to N^{\prime}
.
The derivation
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
is of the form:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash N^{\prime}\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,N^{\prime}:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}}{\texttt{{Llamt}}}

Note that since
N\to N^{\prime}

we have that
N\{a:=A\}\to N^{\prime}\{a:=A\}
.
By i.h. we have that

\mathbf{E}\vdash N\{a:=A\}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
,
hence applying the rule
Llamt

we obtain that

\mathbf{E}\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,N:A\mathop{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\forall$}}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
,
as required.

13.

Verification of an implication,

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N\to{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N^{\prime}=M^{\prime}

with
N\to N^{\prime}
.
The derivation
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
is of the form:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N^{\prime}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\star}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N^{\prime}:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}$}}}

Note that since
N\to N^{\prime}

we have that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A})\to{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N^{\prime}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A})
.
By i.h. we have that

\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}):\star
,
hence applying the rule
L
\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}

we obtain that

\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N:\star

as required.

14.

Verification of a second-order universal quantifier,

M={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N\to{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N^{\prime}=M^{\prime}

with
N\to N^{\prime}
.
The derivation
\Phi\rhd\mathbf{E}\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathbb{T}
is of the form:

\frac{\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N^{\prime}\,\mathbf{a}):\star\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(A,N^{\prime})}{\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N^{\prime}:\star}{\texttt{{L$\langle\rangle{\forall}$}}}

Note that since
N\to N^{\prime}

we have that

\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a})\to\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N^{\prime}\,\mathbf{a})
.
Furthermore, by
\alpha
-renaming we may assume that
\mathbf{a}\notin\mathsf{fv}(N)
.
By i.h. we have that

\mathbf{E}\vdash\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(N\,\mathbf{a}):\star

hence applying the rule
L
\langle\rangle{\Rightarrow}

we obtain that

\mathbf{E}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}N:\star

as required.

15.

The remaning congruence cases are straightforward by i.h.,
similar to the proof of case
10

in this lemma.

∎

Proposition B.13 (Standardization).

If
M\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}\star
then
M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.

Proof.

Suppose that
M\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}\star
.
Note that
\vdash\star:\star
.
Since
M\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}\star
,
by applying subject expansion (
lemma

B.12
)
repeatedly, we obtain a derivation
\Phi_{0}

such that
\Phi_{0}\rhd\vdash M:\star
.
Consider a maximal, potentially infinite,
sequence of weak head reduction steps
M=M_{0}\to_{w}M_{1}\to_{w}M_{2}\ldots
.
Applying weak head subject reduction (
lemma

B.10
)
repeatedly, we construct a sequence of derivations

\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2},\ldots

such that
\Phi_{i}\rhd\vdash M_{i}:\star
for all
i

and such that
\lvert\Phi_{i}\rvert>\lvert\Phi_{i+1}\rvert
for all
i
.
Then the reduction sequence
M=M_{0}\to_{w}M_{1}\to_{w}M_{2}\ldots

must be finite because otherwise we would have an infinite decreasing sequence
of natural numbers

\lvert\Phi_{0}\rvert>\lvert\Phi_{1}\rvert>\lvert\Phi_{2}\rvert>\ldots
.
Let
M_{n}
be the last term of the sequence,
which must be a
\to_{w}
-normal form.
Since
M\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}\star
and
M\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}M_{n}

by confluence (
proposition

4.3
) we have that
M_{n}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}\star
,
but
M_{n}
is a weak head normal form, so
M_{n}=\star
.
∎

B.4.
Second-Order: Soundness (
theorem

4.12
)

The soundness theorem is proved in the main body (
theorem

4.12
).
In this section we prove all the required auxiliary lemmas:

•

Correctness

(
lemma

B.14
)
corresponding to
lemma

4.5
in the main body.

•

Adequacy for types

(
lemma

B.15
),
corresponding to
lemma

4.8
in the main body.

•

Irrelevance

(
lemma

B.16
)
corresponding to
lemma

4.9
in the main body.

•

Substitution lemma

(
lemma

B.17
)
corresponding to
lemma

4.10
in the main body.

Lemma B.14 (Correctness).

If
A
has no free type-variables then

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.

Proof.

By induction on the size of
A
:

1.

Eigenvariable,
A=\mathbf{a}
:
Then
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\to\star
.

2.

Implication,
A=(B\Rightarrow C)
:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A\Rightarrow B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A\Rightarrow B}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A})\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}((\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B})\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A})\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}A{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{A}{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B})\\
&\twoheadrightarrow_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(\star{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B})&\text{by i.h. }\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B}\\
&\twoheadrightarrow_{w}&\star&\text{by i.h. }\end{array}

3.

Second-order universal quantifier,
A=(\forall a.\,B)
:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\forall a.\,B{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,B}\\
&\to_{w}&\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,B}\,\mathbf{a})\\
&\to_{w}&\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}((\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B})\,\mathbf{a})\\
&\to_{w}&\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B\{a:=\mathbf{a}\}}\\
&\twoheadrightarrow_{w}&\nu{\mathbf{a}}.\,\star&\text{by i.h. }\\
&\to_{w}&\star\end{array}

∎

Lemma B.15 (Adequacy for types).

\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{A^{\eta}}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
A
.

If
A
is a
type-variable
(
A=a
):
Let
\eta(a)=(B,X)
, where
we have that
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{B}

by definition of
\eta
being a candidate-assignment.
Note that
\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket a\rrbracket\eta=X
and

A^{\eta}=a^{\eta}=B
,
so to check that
\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{A^{\eta}}

amounts to checking that
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{B}
, which holds by hypothesis.

If
A
is an
eigenvariable
(
A=\mathbf{a}
):
Note that, by definition,

\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta\,\mathrel{:=}\,\{M\ |\ M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\}

and
\mathbf{a}^{\eta}=\mathbf{a}
,
because eigenvariables are not affected by substitutions.
In order to show that
\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{\mathbf{a}}
,
we check each of the three conditions defining
\mathcal{VC}_{\mathbf{a}}
:

1.

In order to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\in\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta
,
note that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
in zero reduction steps.

2.

Let
M\in\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta
.
Then
M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
,
so
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}\mathbf{a}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\to_{w}\star
.

3.

Let
M,M^{\prime}
be metaterms such that
M\to_{w}M^{\prime}
.
Then by confluence (
proposition

4.3
)
we have that
M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}

if and only if
M^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}
,
so
M\in\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta

if and only if
M^{\prime}\in\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta
.

If
A
is an
implication
(
A=(B\Rightarrow C)
):
Note first that
\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{B^{\eta}}

and
\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{C^{\eta}}
by i.h.,
so in particular

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\eta}}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{C^{\eta}}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

by
lemma

4.5
.
In order to show that
\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}
,
we check each of the three conditions defining
\mathcal{VC}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}
:

1.

In order to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta
,
it suffices to show the two conditions defining
\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta
.
By correctness
lemma

4.5
, we have that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
,
so it suffices to show that for every
M\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
we have that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}\,M\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta
.
Indeed, let
M\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
,
and let us show that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}\,M\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta
.
Since
\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{C^{\eta}}
by i.h.,
we know that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{C^{\eta}}\in\mathcal{VC}_{C^{\eta}}

and that
\mathcal{VC}_{C^{\eta}}
is closed by
\to_{w}
-convertibility,
hence it suffices to show that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}\,M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{C^{\eta}}
.
Moreover, since
\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{B^{\eta}}
by i.h.,
we know in particular that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{llll}&&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}}\,M\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\eta}\Rightarrow C^{\eta}}\,M\\
&\to_{w}&(\lambda x.\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}x\,{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{C^{\eta}})\,M\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{C^{\eta}}\\
&\twoheadrightarrow_{w}&\star{?}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{C^{\eta}}&\text{since ${\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star$}\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{C^{\eta}}\end{array}

2.

Let
M\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta

and observe that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

holds by definition of
\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta
.

3.

Let
M
,
M^{\prime}
be metaterms such that
M\to_{w}M^{\prime}
.
We argue that
M\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta

if and only if
M^{\prime}\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta
.

(\Rightarrow)

Suppose that
M\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta
.
By definition, this means that two conditions hold,
first
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

and, second,
M\,N\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta

holds for every
N\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
From the first condition and confluence (
proposition

4.3
)
it is immediate to conclude that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.
Moreover, for each
N\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
,
we have that
M\,N\to_{w}M^{\prime}\,N
,
so by the second condition and the fact that

\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{C^{\eta}}
holds by i.h.,
we obtain that
M^{\prime}\,N\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta
.
Hence
M^{\prime}\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta
.

(\Leftarrow)

Similar to the proof of the
(\Rightarrow)
direction.

If
A
is a
universal quantification
(
A=\forall a.\,B
):
Given an arbitrary type
C
and an arbitrary
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
,
we may consider the candidate-assignment
{\eta*}\,\mathrel{:=}\,\eta[a\mapsto(C,X)]
.
Note by i.h. that in this case we have that

\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}\in\mathcal{VC}_{B^{{\eta*}}}
.

Now, in order to show that

\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta\in\mathcal{VC}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}
,
we check each of the three conditions defining
\mathcal{VC}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}
:

1.

To show that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}\in\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta
,
it suffices to show the two conditions defining
\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta
.
By correctness (
lemma

4.5
), we have that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
,
so it suffices to show that
for every type
C
and every
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}

we have that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}\,C\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(C,X)]
.
Indeed,
let
C
be any type and let
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
,
and let us write
{\eta*}\,\mathrel{:=}\,\eta[a\mapsto(C,X)]
.
Our goal is to show that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}\,C\in\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}
.
Since by i.h. we have that

\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}\in\mathcal{VC}_{B^{{\eta*}}}
,
we know in particular that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{{\eta*}}}\in\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}

and that
\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}
is closed by
\to_{w}
-convertibility,
hence it suffices to show that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}\,C\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{{\eta*}}}
.
Let us write
\sigma\,\mathrel{:=}\,\tilde{\eta}[a\mapsto a]

be the type-substitution corresponding to the first projection
of
\eta
but fixing
a
,
in such a way that

(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}=\forall a.\,B^{\sigma}
.
Then we have:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}}\,C\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\forall a.\,B^{\sigma}}\,C\\
&\to_{w}&(\Lambda{a}.\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\sigma}})\,C\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\sigma}\{a:=C\}}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{{\eta*}}}\end{array}

2.

Let
M\in\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta

and observe that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

holds by definition of
\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta
.

3.

Let
M
,
M^{\prime}
be metaterms such that
M\to_{w}N
.
We argue that
M\in\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta

if and only if
M^{\prime}\in\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta
.

(\Rightarrow)

Suppose that
M\in\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta
.
By definition,
this means that two conditions hold,
first
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

and, second,
M\,C\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(C,X)]

holds for every type
C
and every
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
.
From the first condition and confluence (
proposition

4.3
)
it is immediate to conclude that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.
Moreover, for each type
C
and each
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
,
and writing
{\eta*}\,\mathrel{:=}\,\eta[a\mapsto(C,X)]
,
we have that
M\,C\to_{w}M^{\prime}\,C
,
so by the second condition and the fact that

\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}\in\mathcal{VC}_{B^{{\eta*}}}
holds by i.h. we obtain that
M^{\prime}\,C\in\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}
.
Hence
M^{\prime}\in\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta
.

(\Leftarrow)

Similar to the proof of the
(\Rightarrow)
direction.

∎

Lemma B.16 (Irrelevance).

If
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
agree on the free variables of
A
,
then
\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta_{1}=\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta_{2}
.

Proof.

Straightforward by induction on
A
.
∎

Lemma B.17 (Substitution lemma).

\llbracket A\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]

Proof.

By induction on
A
.

If
A
is the
same type-variable
as
a
,
i.e.

A=a
:
Then

\llbracket a\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket a\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]
.

If
A
is a
different type-variable
than
a
,
i.e.

A=b\neq a
:
Then

\llbracket b\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket b\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket b\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]
.

Eigenvariable,
A=\mathbf{a}
.
Then

\llbracket\mathbf{a}\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta=\{M\ |\ M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{\mathbf{a}}\}=\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]
.

If
A
is an
implication
,
i.e.

A=(A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2})
:
Our goal is to show that

\llbracket(A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2})\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2}\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]

are equal as sets. We show the two inclusions:

(\subseteq)

Let
M\in\llbracket(A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2})\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta
,
that is
M\in\llbracket A_{1}\{a:=B\}\Rightarrow A_{2}\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta
.
This means that two conditions hold,
first

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(A_{1}\{a:=B\}\Rightarrow A_{2}\{a:=B\})^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

and, second,

M\,N\in\llbracket A_{2}\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta

holds for every
N\in\llbracket A_{1}\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta
.
Note that

(A_{1}\{a:=B\}\Rightarrow A_{2}\{a:=B\})^{\eta}=(A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2})\{a:=B\}^{\eta}=(A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2})^{\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]}
,
so the first condition implies that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2})^{\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.
Moreover, by resorting to the i.h., the second condition implies that

M\,N\in\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]

holds for every
N\in\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]
.
Hence

M\in\llbracket(A_{1}\Rightarrow A_{2})\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]
.

(\supseteq)

Symmetric to the proof of the
(\subseteq)
case.

If
A
is a
universal quantification
,
i.e.

A=(\forall b.\,A^{\prime})
:
Assume, using
\alpha
-conversion if necessary, that
b\notin\{a\}\cup\mathsf{fv}(B)
.
Our goal is to show that

\llbracket(\forall b.\,A^{\prime})\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta=\llbracket\forall b.\,A^{\prime}\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]

are equal as sets.
We show the two inclusions:

(\subseteq)

Let
M\in\llbracket(\forall b.\,A^{\prime})\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta
,
that is
M\in\llbracket\forall b.\,A^{\prime}\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta
.
This means that two conditions hold,
first
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(\forall b.\,A^{\prime}\{a:=B\})^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

and, second,

M\,C\in\llbracket A^{\prime}\{a:=B\}\rrbracket\eta[b\mapsto(C,X)]

holds for every type
C
and every
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
.
Note that

(\forall b.\,A^{\prime}\{a:=B\})^{\eta}=(\forall b.\,A^{\prime})\{a:=B\}^{\eta}=(\forall b.\,A^{\prime})^{\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]}
,
so the first condition implies that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(\forall b.\,A^{\prime})^{\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.
Consider an arbitrary type
C
,
an arbitrary
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
,
and let us write
{\eta*}\,\mathrel{:=}\,\eta[b\mapsto(C,X)]
.
By resorting to the i.h., the second condition
implies that

M\,C\in\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket{\eta*}[a\mapsto(B^{{\eta*}},\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*})]
.
Moreover, since
b\notin\mathsf{fv}(B)

we have that

B^{{\eta*}}=B^{\eta}

and

\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}=\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
by irrelevance (
lemma

4.9
).
From this we obtain that

M\,C\in\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket\eta[b\mapsto(C,X)][a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]

holds for every type
C
and every
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
.
Hence

M\in\llbracket(\forall b.\,A^{\prime})\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(B^{\eta},\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta)]
.

(\supseteq)

Symmetric to the proof of the
(\subseteq)
case.

∎

Lemma B.18 (Adequacy for terms).

Let
\Gamma\vdash t:A
and
\Gamma\vDash^{\eta}\gamma
.
Then
t^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of the judgment
\Gamma\vdash t:A
:

Axiom rule:

Let
t=x
and
\Gamma,x:A\vdash x:A
.
Then since
\Gamma\vDash^{\eta}\gamma

we have that

t^{\eta\gamma}=x^{\eta\gamma}=x^{\gamma}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta
.

Introduction of implication:

Let
t=\lambda x.\,s
and
A=B\Rightarrow C
,
and let

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.\,s:B\Rightarrow C

be derived from

\Gamma,x:B\vdash s:C
.
Note that by definition of
\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta
,
in order to show that

(\lambda x.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow C\rrbracket\eta

we have to show two conditions,
first that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(\lambda x.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

and, second,
that
(\lambda x.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\,M\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta

holds for every
M\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
Let us show each of the two conditions:

1.

To show the first condition,
recall that

\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
is a
B^{\eta}
-verification candidate by
lemma

4.8
,
so in particular
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\eta}}\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
Consider the substitution
{\gamma*}\,\mathrel{:=}\,\gamma[x\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\eta}}]

and observe that

\Gamma,x:B\vDash^{\eta}{\gamma*}

because
x^{{\gamma*}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\eta}}\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
Applying the i.h., this implies that

s^{\eta{\gamma*}}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta
,
and since

\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta
is a
C^{\eta}
-verification candidate by
lemma

4.8
,
this means in particular that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}s^{\eta{\gamma*}}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(B\Rightarrow C)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(\lambda x.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}B^{\eta}\Rightarrow C^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(\lambda x.\,s^{\eta\gamma[x\mapsto x]})\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}((\lambda x.\,s^{\eta\gamma[x\mapsto x]}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\eta}})\\
&\to_{w}&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(s^{\eta\gamma[x\mapsto x]}\{x:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\maltese}_{B^{\eta}}\})\\
&=&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}C^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}s^{\eta{\gamma*}}\\
&\twoheadrightarrow_{w}&\star\end{array}

2.

To show the second condition, let
M
be an arbitrary metaterm such that
M\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
Consider the substitution
{\gamma*}\,\mathrel{:=}\,\gamma[x\mapsto M]

and observe that

\Gamma,x:B\vDash^{\eta}{\gamma*}

because
x^{{\gamma*}}=M\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
Applying the i.h., this implies that

s^{\eta{\gamma*}}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta
.
Then we have that:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&(\lambda x.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\,M\\
&=&(\lambda x.\,s^{\eta\gamma[x\mapsto x]})\,M\\
&\to_{w}&s^{\eta\gamma[x\mapsto x]}\{x:=M\}\\
&=&s^{\eta\gamma[x\mapsto M]}\\
&=&s^{\eta{\gamma*}}\end{array}

Since

\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
is a
B^{\eta}
-verification candidate by
lemma

4.8
,
in particular it is closed by
\to_{w}
-convertibility,
and since we have that

(\lambda x.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\,M\twoheadrightarrow_{w}s^{\eta{\gamma*}}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta

we conclude that

(\lambda x.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\,M\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta
.

Elimination of implication:

Let
t=s\,u
,
and let

\Gamma\vdash s\,u:A

be derived from

\Gamma\vdash s:B\Rightarrow A

and

\Gamma\vdash u:B
.
By i.h. we have that

s^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow A\rrbracket\eta

and that

u^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
Moreover, by definition of
\llbracket B\Rightarrow A\rrbracket\eta
,
the fact that

s^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket B\Rightarrow A\rrbracket\eta

entails that

s^{\eta\gamma}\,M\in\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta

holds for every
M\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta
.
In particular,

s^{\eta\gamma}\,u^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta
,

i.e.

t^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket A\rrbracket\eta
.

Introduction of second-order universal quantifier:

Let
t=\Lambda{a}.\,s

and
A=\forall a.\,B
,
and let

\Gamma\vdash\Lambda{a}.\,s:\forall a.\,B

be derived from

\Gamma\vdash s:B
,
where
a\notin\mathsf{fv}(\Gamma)
.
Note that, by definition of
\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta
,
in order to show that

(\Lambda{a}.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\in\llbracket\forall a.\,B\rrbracket\eta

we have to show two conditions,
first that

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\langle}(\forall a.\,B)^{\eta}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rangle}(\Lambda{a}.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\twoheadrightarrow_{w}\star

and, second, that

(\Lambda{a}.\,s)^{\eta\gamma}\,C\in\llbracket B\rrbracket\eta[a\mapsto(C,X)]

for every type
C

and every
X\in\mathcal{VC}_{C}
.

1.

To show the first condition,
begin by considering the candidate-assignment
{\eta*}\,\mathrel{:=}\,\eta[a\mapsto(\mathbf{a},\llbracket\mathbf{a}\rrbracket\eta)]
.
Recall that
\Gamma\vDash^{\eta}\gamma
holds by hypothesis,
and observe that
\Gamma\vDash^{{\eta*}}\gamma
also holds,
because if
x:C\in\Gamma

then by hypothesis
we know that
x^{\gamma}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket\eta

and the application of the introduction rule for
\forall

ensures that
a\notin\mathsf{fv}(C)
;
hence
\eta
and
{\eta*}
agree on the free variables of
C
,
so
x^{\gamma}\in\llbracket C\rrbracket{\eta*}

by irrelevance (
lemma

4.9
).
By i.h. we obtain that
s^{{\eta*}\gamma}\in\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}
,
and since
\llbracket B\rrbracket{\eta*}

is a
B^{{\eta*}}
-verification candidate by
lemma

4.8
,
this means in particular that
